But then I also think about how I still have stuff I’m supposed to do, and my mom would ask why I’m just sitting there, and then I start thinking about dinner or whether my scrubs are washed for the next day.\n\nSo… I’m not sure if that’s the same as life not being worth living. It’s more like everything feels really heavy and I get stuck in my head.\n\nDoctor: Are you having thoughts of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t know. I don’t think I have a clear answer right this second.\n\nDoctor: Right now, do you want to kill yourself?\n\nPatient: Right now… I don’t know. I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly. Like, “enjoy” feels like a bigger word than it should be.

There are things I usually do, or I used to do, but then I start thinking maybe I only liked them because they filled time, you know? What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because I’m just… completely drained. Like my body feels empty. Even walking from my car into the clinic felt like this huge thing, and at work, just opening the EHR feels like it takes everything I’ve got. It’s weird because I’m only 18, you know, and I keep thinking I should have more energy than this—like my coworkers can just move around like normal, and I’m over here feeling like I need to sit down after doing basically nothing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, after my energy just kind of disappeared, I started feeling really empty most days. Like hopeless, I guess. I keep thinking I already messed up my whole future in healthcare before it even got started, which sounds dramatic, but it just sits in my head a lot. Like at work I’ll see people who seem to know what they’re doing, and then I start thinking about school and whether I should’ve done something different, and… yeah. Mostly I’ve been feeling pretty down.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or enjoyment in things you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe? I haven’t really been paying attention to that part. Like, I mostly just notice how hard it is to get through the day, and then I get home and kind of… sit there. I used to have stuff I’d do after work, but lately it’s all blurred together.\n\nI guess I’m not sure if it’s that I don’t enjoy things or if I’m just too tired to even start them. And then I feel bad because people ask what I do for fun, and I’m like, I don’t know, I’m just trying to make it to tomorrow’s shift.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts that life isn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know how to answer that exactly. Like, I’ve had moments where I think, “What’s the point of all this?” especially after work when I’m just sitting in my car way too long before going inside. But then I also think about how I still have stuff I’m supposed to do, and my mom would ask why I’m just sitting there, and then I start thinking about dinner or whether my scrubs are washed for the next day.\n\nSo… I’m not sure if that’s the same as life not being worth living. It’s more like everything feels really heavy and I get stuck in my head.\n\nDoctor: Are you having thoughts of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t know. I mean, I get really stuck thinking everything is ruined, but “killing myself” feels like a huge thing to say out loud.\n\nSometimes my brain goes all over the place, like I’ll be thinking about work and then my car insurance and then how I used to think I’d have everything figured out after high school, which is stupid because nobody does, right? But then it all kind of turns into this heavy feeling.\n\nSo… I’m not sure how to answer it. I don’t think I have a clear answer right this second.\n\nDoctor: Right now, do you want to kill yourself?\n\nPatient: Right now… I don’t know. I keep trying to answer it cleanly, like yes or no, but my head doesn’t really do that.\n\nI’m sitting here thinking about how if I say yes, then everything becomes a whole thing, and if I say no, I don’t know if that’s true either. Like when people ask at work if you’re “okay,” nobody actually wants the whole answer, they just want you to say yeah and keep moving. And I’m used to doing that, just putting stuff in the right drawer and moving on.\n\nSo… right now, I don’t know if I want to kill myself. I just feel really overwhelmed and kind of scared to answer wrong.\n\nDoctor: At this moment, do you want to die?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. “Want to die” sounds so final, and my brain keeps trying to compare it to other stuff, like when you’re so tired after a shift that you just want everything to stop for a minute, but that’s not the same thing, I guess.\n\nI keep thinking about how people say things like “I’m dead tired” and they don’t mean it, and now I’m wondering if I mean it or if I’m just saying it wrong. I’m not trying to be difficult. I just… I don’t have a clean answer right this second.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any unintentional weight change lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t have a number, because I don’t really weigh myself, but my scrubs started feeling looser, like hanging off me more.\n\nI just wasn’t really eating. I’d skip lunch and then dinner too, and then realize I’d basically only had coffee or water all day. Which is dumb because at work I’m around food trays and break rooms and people talking about what they’re ordering, and I’d be like, yeah, I’ll eat later, and then later just never happened.\n\nSo… yeah, I think I lost weight without meaning to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this down nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it’s been most days. I’m bad with counting days exactly, because everything kind of blurs together, like work weeks and days off and whether I did laundry or just thought about doing it.\n\nBut after all that energy kind of dropped out of me, I started feeling really empty. Like hopeless, most days. And I keep thinking I already messed up my whole future in healthcare before it even really started, which sounds dramatic when I say it out loud, but it feels true in my head.\n\nSo… yeah, nearly every day, I think. For at least a couple weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly. Like, “enjoy” feels like a bigger word than it should be.\n\nThere are things I usually do, or I used to do, but then I start thinking maybe I only liked them because they filled time, you know? Like how people put shows on in the background and then say it’s their favorite show, but they didn’t actually watch half of it. I do that sometimes and then I can’t tell if I enjoyed it or just… had noise on.\n\nSo I don’t know. I haven’t really been paying attention to whether I’m enjoying things. I guess I’d have to think about what counts.\n\nDoctor: Does resting or sleeping make your exhaustion feel better?\n\nPatient: Not really. But then it all kind of turns into this heavy feeling.\n\nSo… I’m not sure how to answer it. I don’t think I have a clear answer right this second.\n\nDoctor: Right now, do you want to kill yourself?\n\nPatient: Right now… I don’t know. I keep trying to answer it cleanly, like yes or no, but my head doesn’t really do that.\n\nI’m sitting here thinking about how if I say yes, then everything becomes a whole thing, and if I say no, I don’t know if that’s true either. And I’m used to doing that, just putting stuff in the right drawer and moving on.\n\nSo… right now, I don’t know if I want to kill myself. It’s more like everything feels really heavy and I get stuck in my head.\n\nDoctor: Are you having thoughts of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t know. I mean, I get really stuck thinking everything is ruined, but “killing myself” feels like a huge thing to say out loud.\n\nSometimes my brain goes all over the place, like I’ll be thinking about work and then my car insurance and then how I used to think I’d have everything figured out after high school, which is stupid because nobody does, right? But then it all kind of turns into this heavy feeling.\n\nSo… I’m not sure how to answer it. I don’t think I have a clear answer right this second.\n\nDoctor: Right now, do you want to kill yourself?\n\nPatient: Right now… I don’t know. I keep trying to answer it cleanly, like yes or no, but my head doesn’t really do that.\n\nI’m sitting here thinking about how if I say yes, then everything becomes a whole thing, and if I say no, I don’t know if that’s true either. Like when people ask at work if you’re “okay,” nobody actually wants the whole answer, they just want you to say yeah and keep moving. And I’m used to doing that, just putting stuff in the right drawer and moving on.\n\nSo… right now, I don’t know if I want to kill myself. I just feel really overwhelmed and kind of scared to answer wrong.\n\nDoctor: At this moment, do you want to die?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. “Want to die” sounds so final, and my brain keeps trying to compare it to other stuff, like when you’re so tired after a shift that you just want everything to stop for a minute, but that’s not the same thing, I guess.\n\nI keep thinking about how people say things like “I’m dead tired” and they don’t mean it, and now I’m wondering if I mean it or if I’m just saying it wrong. I’m not trying to be difficult. I just… I don’t have a clean answer right this second.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any unintentional weight change lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t have a number, because I don’t really weigh myself, but my scrubs started feeling looser, like hanging off me more.\n\nI just wasn’t really eating. I’d skip lunch and then dinner too, and then realize I’d basically only had coffee or water all day. Which is dumb because at work I’m around food trays and break rooms and people talking about what they’re ordering, and I’d be like, yeah, I’ll eat later, and then later just never happened.\n\nSo… yeah, I think I lost weight without meaning to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this down nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it’s been most days. I’m bad with counting days exactly, because everything kind of blurs together, like work weeks and days off and whether I did laundry or just thought about doing it.\n\nBut after all that energy kind of dropped out of me, I started feeling really empty. Like hopeless, most days. And I keep thinking I already messed up my whole future in healthcare before it even really started, which sounds dramatic when I say it out loud, but it feels true in my head.\n\nSo… yeah, nearly every day, I think. For at least a couple weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly. Like, “enjoy” feels like a bigger word than it should be.\n\nThere are things I usually do, or I used to do, but then I start thinking maybe I only liked them because they filled time, you know? Like how people put shows on in the background and then say it’s their favorite show, but they didn’t actually watch half of it. I do that sometimes and then I can’t tell if I enjoyed it or just… had noise on.\n\nSo I don’t know. I haven’t really been paying attention to whether I’m enjoying things. I guess I’d have to think about what counts.\n\nDoctor: Does resting or sleeping make your exhaustion feel better?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I’ll lie down and it’s like my body is still completely drained when I get back up.\n\nThat’s honestly kind of why I’m here. Even small stuff feels ridiculous, like walking from my car to the clinic, I’m thinking, why does this feel like a hike? Or at work, just opening the EHR and getting started feels like it takes everything I have.\n\nSleep doesn’t feel like it refills anything. It’s more like I’m just… paused for a while, and then I’m still exhausted.\n\nDoctor: After even small physical or mental effort, do you feel much worse afterward?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Like, even tiny stuff feels like it wipes me out more than it should.\n\nWalking from my car to here, I was already thinking, why does this feel like such a whole thing? And at work, it’s weird, because opening the EHR should be nothing, it’s just clicking around, but sometimes it feels like I’m staring at it like it’s a wall I have to climb.\n\nI don’t know if that’s exactly what you mean by “much worse afterward,” but my body feels completely drained, like there’s just nothing in the tank.\n\nDoctor: After you push yourself even a little, do you usually crash later?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… I don’t know if “crash” is the exact word, but after I push even a little, it feels like my body just has nothing left.\n\nLike even getting from my car into the clinic felt like way more than it should’ve. And at work, opening the EHR—something that’s literally just clicking around—can feel like it takes everything I have. I keep thinking it shouldn’t be that hard, but it’s like I’m completely drained before I even start.\n\nDoctor: When you wake up after a full night of sleep, do you still feel exhausted?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even after a full night, I still feel wiped out. Like I’ll wake up and it’s not like I’m rested, it’s more like my body’s already empty before the day starts.\n\nThat’s kind of the whole reason I came in, honestly. Even walking from my car to the clinic felt like way too much, and at work just opening the EHR can feel like it takes everything I’ve got. It sounds stupid because it’s not even hard, it’s just… clicking things, but I feel completely drained.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually sensitive to cold?\n\nPatient: Um… cold? I don’t know. I mean, I get cold sometimes, like if the room is freezing or the AC is on too high, but I haven’t really noticed it being unusual.\n\nAt work they keep some areas so cold, and then someone will say it’s normal temperature, so I don’t know if that’s me or just the building. But no, I don’t think I’ve been, like, suddenly way more sensitive to cold or anything.\n\nDoctor: Is your mood usually worst in the morning?\n\nPatient: I guess… mornings can be bad, yeah, but it’s more like most days I wake up and there’s already this empty feeling there.\n\nAfter my energy dropped off like this, I just started feeling hopeless a lot, like I’d already messed up my whole future in healthcare before I even really got started. So yeah, mornings aren’t great, but it kind of follows me around most of the day.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. But no, I don’t think I’ve been, like, suddenly way more sensitive to cold or anything.\n\nDoctor: Is your mood usually worst in the morning?\n\nPatient: I guess… mornings can be bad, yeah, but it’s more like most days I wake up and there’s already this empty feeling there.\n\nAfter my energy dropped off like this, I just started feeling hopeless a lot, like I’d already messed up my whole future in healthcare before I even really got started. Which sounds dramatic, I know, because I’m only 18, but when I’m at work and I can barely get myself to do basic stuff, it feels like… what am I even doing here? So yeah, mornings aren’t great, but it kind of follows me around most of the day.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you wished you were dead?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 18 / healthcare_support_worker / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I… I don’t know how to answer that exactly. Like, I don’t sit there with those words in my head all the time or anything.

Sometimes I just get really tired of everything, and then I start thinking about how people at work seem to handle normal stuff so easily, and I’m over here struggling just to keep up, and then I spiral about whether I’m even supposed to be doing healthcare at all. My mom keeps saying everyone feels overwhelmed starting out, but it doesn’t really feel like that when I’m in it.

So… I’m not sure. I guess I’ve had moments where I don’t really want to deal with being here, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as wishing I was dead.

I don’t think I have a clear answer right this second.\n\nDoctor: Right now, do you want to kill yourself?\n\nPatient: Right now… I don’t know. Like hopeless, most days. And I keep thinking I already messed up my whole future in healthcare before it even really started, which sounds dramatic when I say it out loud, but it feels true in my head.\n\nSo… yeah, nearly every day, I think. For at least a couple weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly. Like, “enjoy” feels like a bigger word than it should be.\n\nThere are things I usually do, or I used to do, but then I start thinking maybe I only liked them because they filled time, you know? Like how people put shows on in the background and then say it’s their favorite show, but they didn’t actually watch half of it. I do that sometimes and then I can’t tell if I enjoyed it or just… had noise on.\n\nSo I don’t know. I haven’t really been paying attention to whether I’m enjoying things. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… I don’t know. Sometimes I think people would probably have an easier time if I wasn’t messing everything up, but then I start thinking about work and how everyone else seems to know what they’re doing, and then I just feel stupid for even saying it out loud.

So… maybe in a way, yeah, but I don’t know if I mean dead exactly. It’s more like I don’t want to be this person who can’t keep up.

It’s more like everything feels really heavy and I get stuck in my head.\n\nDoctor: Are you having thoughts of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t know. But then it all kind of turns into this heavy feeling.\n\nSo… I’m not sure how to answer it. I don’t think I have a clear answer right this second.\n\nDoctor: Right now, do you want to kill yourself?\n\nPatient: Right now… I don’t know. I keep trying to answer it cleanly, like yes or no, but my head doesn’t really do that.\n\nI’m sitting here thinking about how if I say yes, then everything becomes a whole thing, and if I say no, I don’t know if that’s true either. Like when people ask at work if you’re “okay,” nobody actually wants the whole answer, they just want you to say yeah and keep moving. And I’m used to doing that, just putting stuff in the right drawer and moving on.\n\nSo… right now, I don’t know if I want to kill myself. I just feel really overwhelmed and kind of scared to answer wrong.\n\nDoctor: At this moment, do you want to die?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. “Want to die” sounds so final, and my brain keeps trying to compare it to other stuff, like when you’re so tired after a shift that you just want everything to stop for a minute, but that’s not the same thing, I guess.\n\nI keep thinking about how people say things like “I’m dead tired” and they don’t mean it, and now I’m wondering if I mean it or if I’m just saying it wrong. I’m not trying to be difficult. I just… I don’t have a clean answer right this second.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any unintentional weight change lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Like hopeless, most days. And I keep thinking I already messed up my whole future in healthcare before it even really started, which sounds dramatic when I say it out loud, but it feels true in my head.\n\nSo… yeah, nearly every day, I think. Like, “enjoy” feels like a bigger word than it should be.\n\nThere are things I usually do, or I used to do, but then I start thinking maybe I only liked them because they filled time, you know? Like how people put shows on in the background and then say it’s their favorite show, but they didn’t actually watch half of it. I do that sometimes and then I can’t tell if I enjoyed it or just… had noise on.\n\nSo I don’t know. I haven’t really been paying attention to whether I’m enjoying things. I guess I’d have to think about what counts.\n\nDoctor: Does resting or sleeping make your exhaustion feel better?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I’ll lie down and it’s like my body is still completely drained when I get back up.\n\nThat’s honestly kind of why I’m here. Even small stuff feels ridiculous, like walking from my car to the clinic, I’m thinking, why does this feel like a hike? Or at work, just opening the EHR and getting started feels like it takes everything I have.\n\nSleep doesn’t feel like it refills anything. It’s more like I’m just… paused for a while, and then I’m still exhausted.\n\nDoctor: After even small physical or mental effort, do you feel much worse afterward?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Like, even tiny stuff feels like it wipes me out more than it should.\n\nWalking from my car to here, I was already thinking, why does this feel like such a whole thing? And at work, it’s weird, because opening the EHR should be nothing, it’s just clicking around, but sometimes it feels like I’m staring at it like it’s a wall I have to climb.\n\nI don’t know if that’s exactly what you mean by “much worse afterward,” but my body feels completely drained, like there’s just nothing in the tank.\n\nDoctor: After you push yourself even a little, do you usually crash later?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… I don’t know if “crash” is the exact word, but after I push even a little, it feels like my body just has nothing left.\n\nLike even getting from my car into the clinic felt like way more than it should’ve. And at work, opening the EHR—something that’s literally just clicking around—can feel like it takes everything I have. I keep thinking it shouldn’t be that hard, but it’s like I’m completely drained before I even start.\n\nDoctor: When you wake up after a full night of sleep, do you still feel exhausted?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even after a full night, I still feel wiped out. Even walking from my car to the clinic felt like way too much, and at work just opening the EHR can feel like it takes everything I’ve got. It sounds stupid because it’s not even hard, it’s just… clicking things, but I feel completely drained.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually sensitive to cold?\n\nPatient: Um… cold?
